Suppression of blow-up for the 3D Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes system via the Couette flow
Shikun Cui, Lili Wang, Wendong Wang

Abstract
. As is well known, for the 3D Patlak-Keller-Segel system, regardless of whether they are parabolic-elliptic or parabolic-parabolic forms, finite-time blow-up may occur for arbitrarily small values of the initial mass. In this paper, it is proved for the first time that one can prevent the finite-time blow-up when the initial mass is less than a certain critical threshold via the stabilizing effect of the moving Navier-Stokes flows. In details, we investigate the nonlinear stability of the Couette flow ( A ​ y , 0 , 0 ) (Ay,0,0) in the Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes system and show that if the Couette flow is sufficiently strong (A is large enough), then the solutions for Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes system are global in time provided that the initial velocity is sufficiently small and the initial cell mass is less than 24 5 ​ π 2 \frac{24}{5}\pi^{2} .



Keywords:
suppression of blow-up; Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes; stability;
Couette flow

1.
Introduction

Consider the following three-dimensional parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel (PKS) system coupled with Navier-Stokes equations in
(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}
with
\mathbb{T}=[0,2\pi]
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+v\cdot\nabla n=\triangle n-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-c=0,\\
\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla v+\nabla P=\triangle v+n\nabla\phi,\\
\nabla\cdot v=0,\end{array}\right.

(1.1)

along with initial conditions

(n,v)\big|_{t=0}=(n_{\rm in},v_{\rm in}),

where
n
represents the cell density,
c
denotes the chemoattractant density, and
v
denotes the velocity of fluid. In addition,
P
is the pressure and
\phi
is the given potential function.

If
v=0
and
\phi=0
, the system (
1.1
) is reduced to the classical 3D parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel system, which is a mathematical model used to describe the diffusion and chemotactic movement of chemical substances in a population of cells (or microorganisms),
and it was jointly developed by Patlak
[
44
]
, Keller and Segel
[
31
]
.
This system has wide applications in the fields of biology, ecology, and medicine. It helps us understand phenomena such as cell migration, aggregation, and diffusion.
As long as the dimension of space is higher than one, the solutions of the classical PKS system may blow up in finite time.
In the 2D space, the parabolic-parabolic PKS model (
\triangle c
is replaced by
\triangle c-\partial_{t}c
in
\eqref{ini}_{2}
) has a critical mass of
8\pi
, if the cell mass
M:=||n_{\rm in}||_{L^{1}}
is less than
8\pi
, the solutions of the system are global in time proved by Calvez-Corrias
[
9
]
,
if the cell mass is greater than
8\pi
, the solutions will blow up in finite time proved by Schweyer
[
46
]
.
In the 2D space, the parabolic-elliptic PKS system is globally well-posed if and only if the total mass
M\leq 8\pi
by Wei
[
52
]
.
When the spatial dimension is higher than two, the PKS system is supercritical and its solutions will blow up for any initial mass, see Nagai
[
41
]
or Souplet-Winkler
[
48
]
for the parabolic-elliptic case, and see Winkler
[
56
]
for the parabolic-parabolic case. For more results on this topic, we refer to
[
8
,
16
,
50
]
and the references therein. As said in
[
57
]
:

An interesting question is to consider whether the stabilizing effect of the moving fluid can suppress the finite time blow-up?

Firstly,
let us recall some of the results obtained in 2D briefly.
For the parabolic-elliptic PKS system of
(\ref{ini})_{1}-(\ref{ini})_{2}
, Kiselev-Xu
[
32
]
suppressed the blow-up by stationary relaxation
enhancing flows and time-dependent Yao-Zlatos flows in
\mathbb{T}^{d}
with
d=2,3
.
Bedrossian-He
[
3
]
also studied the suppression of blow-up by non-degenerate shear flows (
v\cdot\nabla n=Au(y)\partial_{x}n
) in

\mathbb{T}^{2}
for the 2D parabolic-elliptic case. He
[
22
]
investigated the suppression of blow-up by a large strictly monotone shear flow for the parabolic-parabolic PKS model in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
when
A
lies in an interval. For the coupled PKS-NS system, Zeng-Zhang-Zi
[
57
]
firstly considered the 2D PKS-NS system near the Couette flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
, and
proved that if the Couette flow is sufficiently strong, the solution stays globally regular.
He
[
23
]
considered the blow-up suppression for the parabolic-elliptic PKS-NS system in

\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
with the coupling of buoyancy effects for a class of initial data with small vorticity.
The suppression of blow-up in PKS-NS system via the Poiseuille flow was obtained by
Li-Xiang-Xu
[
33
]
in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
and Cui-Wang in
[
14
]

\mathbb{T}\times I
, respectively. Besides, Hu
[
26
]
proved the solution remains regular for all times in the regime of sufficiently
large buoyancy and viscosity, see also the recent results by Hu-Kiselev-Yao
[
28
]
and Hu-Kiselev
[
27
]
.

For the 3D PKS system of parabolic-elliptic case, Bedrossian-He
[
3
]
investigated the suppression of blow-up by shear flows in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
and
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the initial mass
M<8\pi
.
Feng-Shi-Wang
[
20
]
used the planar helical flows as transport
flow to research the advective Kuramoto-Sivashinsky and
Keller-Segel equations.
Shi-Wang
[
47
]
considered the suppression effect of the flow
(z,z^{2},0)
in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, and Deng-Shi-Wang
[
17
]
proved the Couette flow with a sufficiently large amplitude prevents
the blow-up of solutions in the whole space for exponential decay data.
For the parabolic-parabolic PKS system, He
[
24
]
considered an alternating flow and proved the solution remains globally regular in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
as long as the
flow is sufficiently strong without a mass threshold.
For a time-dependent shear flow, He
[
25
]
demonstrated that when the total
mass of the cell density is below a specific threshold (
8\pi|\mathbb{T}|
), the solution remains globally regular in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
as long as the
flow is sufficiently strong.

For the 3D PKS-NS system, the following questions are still open:

1. Whether the solutions are globally well-posed provided that the amplitude of shear flows is sufficiently large;

2. Whether there exists a critical threshold for the total mass of the cell density.

Note that the critical threshold may depend on the shear flow or the domain. Recently, the authors
[
15
]
investigated the linear stability of the Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
in the 3D PKS system coupled with the linearized NS equations. However, it’s still unknown for the 3D PKS-NS system. We will investigate this issue in this paper.
At this time, this is related the stability problem of the 3D Navier-Stokes equations:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}u-\frac{1}{Re}\triangle u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P=0,\\
\nabla\cdot u=0.\end{array}\right.

(1.2)

Due to Reynolds’s famous work in 1883
[
45
]
, the stability and transition
to turbulence of the laminar flows at high Reynolds number have been an important
field in fluid mechanics. It is well-known that the plane
Couette flow is linearly stable for any Reynolds number
[
19
]
. However,
the experiments show that it can be unstable and transition to turbulence for
small but finite perturbations at high Reynolds number
[
10
]
.
Then an important mathematical question
formulated by Bedrossian, Germain, and
Masmoudi
[
1
]
is that:

Given a norm

\|\cdot\|_{X}
, find a
\beta=\beta(X)
so that

~\|u_{\rm in}\|_{X}\leq Re^{-\beta}\Longrightarrow{\rm stability},

\quad\|u_{\rm in}\|_{X}\gg Re^{-\beta}\Longrightarrow{\rm instability}.

The exponent
\beta
is referred to as the transition threshold in the applied literature.

Bedrossian-Germain-Masmoudi proved
\beta\leq\frac{3}{2}
for the 3D Couette flow
[
1
]
in Sobolev space and
\beta\leq 1
in Gevrey class
[
2
]
.
In Sobolev space, we refer to Wei-Zhang
[
53
]
and Chen-Wei-Zhang
[
13
]
for recent results of
\beta\leq 1
. More references on MHD, Boussinesq equations or other models, we refer to
[
29
,
30
,
34
,
43
,
58
]
and the references therein.
For the stability of the 2D Navier-Stokes equations and related models, there are very rich research progress on this topic, and we refer to
[
5
,
7
,
54
,
39
,
18
,
4
]
and the references therein.

Motivated by the above transition threshold problem
[
1
]
and
[
53
]
, our main goal is to investigate the suppression of blow-up and the nonlinear stability of the system (
1.1
) via the 3D Couette flow. Introduce a perturbation
u=(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})
around the Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
, which
u(t,x,y,z)=v(t,x,y,z)-(Ay,0,0)
satisfying
u\big|_{t=0}=u_{\rm in}=(u_{1,\rm in},u_{2,\rm in},u_{3,\rm in})
. Assume
\phi=y
. Then we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+Ay\partial_{x}n+u\cdot\nabla n-\triangle n=-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-c=0,\\
\partial_{t}u+Ay\partial_{x}u+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}Au_{2}\\
0\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)-\triangle u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P^{N_{1}}+\nabla P^{N_{2}}+\nabla P^{N_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
n\\
0\\
\end{array}\right),\\
\nabla\cdot u=0,\end{array}\right.

(1.3)

where the pressure
P^{N_{1}}
,
P^{N_{2}}
and
P^{N_{3}}
are determined by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\triangle P^{N_{1}}=-2A\partial_{x}u_{2},\\
\triangle P^{N_{2}}=\partial_{y}n,\\
\triangle P^{N_{3}}=-{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u).\end{array}\right.

(1.4)

After the time rescaling
t\mapsto\frac{t}{A}
, we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n+\frac{1}{A}u\cdot\nabla n-\frac{1}{A}\triangle n=-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-c=0,\\
\partial_{t}u+y\partial_{x}u+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{2}\\
0\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u+\frac{1}{A}u\cdot\nabla u+\frac{1}{A}(\nabla P^{N_{1}}+\nabla P^{N_{2}}+\nabla P^{N_{3}})=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
\frac{n}{A}\\
0\\
\end{array}\right),\\
{\nabla\cdot u=0.}\end{array}\right.

(1.5)

Before stating the result, define the following modes

\displaystyle P_{0}f=f_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(t,x,y,z)dx,~~~\qquad\qquad\ P_{\neq}f=f_{\neq}=f-f_{0},

\displaystyle P_{(0,0)}f=f_{(0,0)}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}}f(t,x,y,z)dxdz,\quad P_{(0,\neq)}f=f_{(0,\neq)}=f_{0}-f_{(0,0)}.

Throughout this paper,
f_{0}
and
f_{\neq}
respectively represent the zero mode and non-zero mode of
f
. Moreover,
f_{(0,0)}
and
f_{(0,\neq)}
denote the z-part zero mode and the z-part non-zero mode of
f_{0}
,
respectively.

Our first theorem is stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Assume that
u_{\rm in}(x,y,z)\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
.
Moreover, the non-negative initial data
n_{\rm in}(x,y,z)=n_{\rm in}(x,y)\in H^{2}\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
and

M=\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}n_{\rm in}dxdydz<\frac{24}{5}\pi^{2}.

Then there exists a positive constant
C_{(0)}
depending on
||n_{\rm in}||_{H^{2}\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}

and
||u_{\rm in}||_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}
, such that if
A>C_{(0)}
and
A^{\epsilon_{0}}\|u_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}\leq C
, the solutions of
(
1.5
)
are global in time,
where
\epsilon_{0}
is a positive constant satisfying
\epsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{3}
.

In fact, the above result is an immediate corollary of the following theorem for the general initial data
n_{\rm in}
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Assume that the non-negative initial data
n_{\rm in}(x,y,z)\in H^{2}\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})

and
u_{\rm in}(x,y,z)\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
.
Then there exists a positive constant
C_{(0)}
depending on
||n_{\rm in}||_{H^{2}\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}

and
||u_{\rm in}||_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}
, such that if
A>C_{(0)}
, and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}A^{\epsilon_{0}}\|u_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}+A^{\epsilon_{0}}\|(n_{\rm in})_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}\leq C,\\
\\
M=\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}n_{\rm in}dxdydz<\frac{24}{5}\pi^{2},\end{array}\right.

the solutions of
(
1.5
)
are global in time, where
\epsilon_{0}
is a positive constant satisfying
\epsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{3}
.

Here are some remarks for the above results.

Remark 1.1
.

The suppression of blow-up for the 3D PKS-NS system near the Couette flow was obtained in Theorem
1.2
, which seems to be the first result for the 3D PKS-NS system. For the 2D PKS-NS system, stability analysis or suppression of blow-up was obtained in many references, such as
[
14
,
23
,
33
,
51
,
57
]
, where enhanced dissipation plays a crucial role in stabilizing solutions or suppressing blow-up. However, there are significant differences in complexity and structures between the 2D and 3D PKS-NS systems. For the 3D cases, one needs a comprehensive consideration of multiple effects, including enhanced dissipation, inviscid damping, the 3D lift-up effect, nonlinear interactions and energy transfer mechanisms etc., which bring substantial challenges. For finite channels, the boundary layer effect can also cause some trouble, and we refer to some recent works
[
12
,
11
,
13
]
.

Remark 1.2
.

It’s an open question that whether there exists a sharp
threshold for initial cell mass
M
in the 3D PKS-NS system.
We guess it seems to be
16\pi^{2}
.
In fact, for the 3D PKS system without coupling the Navier-Stokes flow, the zero mode
n_{0}
satisfies

\partial_{t}n_{0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle n_{0}=-\frac{1}{A}\left[\nabla\cdot(n_{\neq}\nabla c_{\neq})_{0}+\partial_{y}(n_{0}\partial_{y}c_{0})+\partial_{z}(n_{0}\partial_{z}c_{0})\right]

and it is similar as the 2D PKS system except for
\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n_{\neq}\nabla c_{\neq})_{0}
as a perturbation.
Recently, He in
[
25
]
proved the well-posed result of
M<16\pi^{2}
for the time-dependent shear flow, where the free energy functional of the zero mode plays an important role. Moreover, this critical value limit can be removed when considering other special flows, such as stationary relaxation
enhancing flows and time-dependent Yao-Zlatos flows in
[
32
]
, and an alternating flow in
[
24
]
.

For the 3D PKS-NS system, there are coupled terms
\partial_{y}(u_{2,0}n_{0})
and
\partial_{z}(u_{3,0}n_{0})
for the zero mode
n_{0}
, which cannot be ignored. Especially, the estimates of
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}
still depend on
n_{0}
, and for more details we refer to the estimates of
T_{1,4}
and
T_{1,5}
in Lemma
4.3
.
Hence, the value of the critical mass threshold for the 3D PKS-NS system is still unknown.

Remark 1.3
.

It is very challenging to remove the restrictions on

\|(u_{\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}
and
\|(n_{\rm in})_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}
due to the 3D lift-up effect and the energy transfer mechanism.
In fact, it follows from (
2.2
) that
u_{2,0}
needs to be small, since
\widehat{u_{1,0}}
should be small by the space-time estimates (Prop
A.3
,
A.4
).

Moreover,
u_{2,0}
and

u_{3,0}
are related to
n_{0}
by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}u_{2,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{2,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{2}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{3}}_{0}=\frac{n_{0}}{A},\\
\partial_{t}u_{3,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{3,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{2}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{3}}_{0}=0,\end{array}\right.

which imply that the norm of
n_{0}
needs to be small to close the energy estimate.

Remark 1.4
.

For a three-dimensional domain with physical boundary, the stability or instability of the PKS-NS system is still open. In a recent work, the authors investigated the linear stability of the Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
in
[
15
]
.
An interesting question is whether one can prove the nonlinear stability of PKS-NS system in a bounded domain.

Here are some notations used in this paper.

Notations
:

•

For given
f
, the Fourier transform can be defined by

f(t,x,y,z)=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{k_{2}\in\mathbb{R}}\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{ik_{2}y}dk_{2}{\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)},

where
\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t)=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{f}(t,x,y,z){\rm e}^{-ik_{2}y}dy{\rm e}^{-i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)}dxdz
.

•

For any given function
f
, the zero mode and the non-zero mode
are defined by
f_{0}
and
f_{\neq}.

Especially, we use
u_{j,0}
, and
u_{j,\neq}
to represent the zero mode
and non-zero mode of the velocity
u_{j}(j=1,2,3)
, respectively.
Similarly, we use
\omega_{2,0}
and
\omega_{2,\neq}
to represent the zero mode
and non-zero mode of the vorticity
\omega_{2}
, respectively.

•

The z-part zero and z-part non-zero modes for
f_{0}
are defined by

f_{(0,0)}
and
f_{(0,\neq)}.

Similarly, we use
u_{j,(0,0)}
, and
u_{j,(0,\neq)}
to represent the z-part zero mode
and z-part non-zero mode of the velocity
u_{j,0}(j=1,2,3)
, respectively.

•

We denote the partial derivatives
\partial_{x},

\partial_{y},
and
\partial_{z}
by

\partial_{1},

\partial_{2},
and
\partial_{3},
respectively.

•

The norm of the
L^{p}
space is defined by

\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}|f|^{p}dxdydz\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the standard
L^{2}
scalar product. For simplicity, write
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}
as
\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

•

The time-space norm
\|f\|_{L^{q}L^{p}}
is defined by

\|f\|_{L^{q}L^{p}}=\big\|\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}\ \big\|_{L^{q}(0,t)}.

•

We define the following norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{a}}^{2}=\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\triangle^{-1}\partial_{x}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Y_{0}}^{2}=\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\frac{1}{A}\|\nabla f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}},

where
a
is a positive constant.

•

The total mass
\|n(t)\|_{L^{1}}
is denoted by
M.
Clearly,
M:=\|n(t)\|_{L^{1}}=\|n_{\rm in}\|_{L^{1}}.

•

Throughout this paper, we denote
C
by a positive constant independent of
A
,
t
and the initial data, and it may be different from line to line.

2.
Key ingredients in the proof

For the 2D PKS-NS system, the enhanced dissipation plays an
important role in stabilizing solutions and suppressing blow-up (for example, see
[
23
,
57
]
).
In 2D finite channel, one needs to consider the impact of the boundary layer effect in addition to the enhanced dissipation (for example, see
[
14
]
).
For the 3D PKS system in either unbounded or bounded domains (for example, see
[
3
,
47
]
), it is necessary to consider the enhanced dissipation and the boundary layer effect, which are similar to the 2D case. It should be noted that the zero mode of
n
is dependent on
y
and
z
in the 3D case, which is different from the 2D case and will bring new difficulties to estimate
\|n_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}.

Generally, it’s more complex in the 3D PKS-NS system, and one needs to address other complex phenomena such as the 3D lift-up effect, nonlinear interactions, and energy transfer mechanisms etc., all of which contribute to the system’s behavior and stability. It is necessary to fully understand the connections between these factors in order to analyze this system clearly, which are stated as follows.

2.1.
Some mechanisms affecting the nonlinear stability

\bullet

3D lift-up effect.

Consider a simplified equation for the zero mode part of the velocity

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}u_{0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{0}+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{2,0}\\
0\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)=0,\\
\nabla\cdot u_{0}=0.\end{array}\right.

The solution of this linear problem is given by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}u_{1,0}(t)=e^{{A}^{-1}t\triangle}\big((u_{1,{\rm in}})_{0}-t(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\big),\\
u_{2,0}(t)=e^{{A}^{-1}t\triangle}(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0},\\
u_{3,0}(t)=e^{{A}^{-1}t\triangle}(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}.\end{array}\right.

When
t\lesssim A,
there is linearized growth for
u_{1,0}(t)
, which is called the “3D lift-up effect”. It is an important factor leading to the 3D instability in the PKS-NS system and more details we refer to
[
1
]
or
[
53
]
.

\bullet

Nonlinear interactions

In 3D space, there are complex nonlinear interactions among different modes of the solution, which influence the stability of the system.
For a given function
f
, we decompose
f
into
f=f_{\neq}+f_{(0,0)}+f_{(0,\neq)},
then nonlinear interactions can be classified as follows:

•

\neq\cdot\neq\rightarrow~\neq~{\rm or}~0,

•

0~\cdot\neq\rightarrow~\neq,

•

0\cdot 0\rightarrow~0,

•

(0,\neq)\cdot(0,\neq)\rightarrow~(0,\neq)~{\rm or}~(0,0),

•

(0,0)\cdot(0,\neq)\rightarrow~(0,\neq),

•

(0,0)\cdot(0,0)\rightarrow~(0,0).

These nonlinear interactions can be directly observed with the help of Fourier series.

\bullet

Energy transfer mechanisms

In the PKS-NS system, energy transfer mechanisms exist either between the cell density and the velocity field or within each of them individually. These mechanisms, involving both linear and nonlinear interactions, directly impact the nonlinear stability of the system.
Therefore, analyzing these transfer mechanisms is essential for understanding and predicting the stability of the system.

Energy transfer mechanisms are classified as follows:

•

Linear transfer between the same Fourier modes,

•

Nonlinear transfer between the same Fourier modes,

•

Nonlinear transfer between the different Fourier modes.

It is crucial to note that there is no linear energy transfer between different modes, which is important for us to estimate the zero modes.

2.2.
Main ideas for constructing the energy functional

\bullet

Constructing the energy functional of zero modes:

Firstly, the zero modes for
u_{2}
and
u_{3}
satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}u_{2,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{2,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{2}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{3}}_{0}=\frac{n_{0}}{A},\\
\partial_{t}u_{3,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{3,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{2}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{3}}_{0}=0,\\
\partial_{y}u_{2,0}+\partial_{z}u_{3,0}=0.\end{array}\right.

Thus
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}
can be regarded as only affected by
n_{(0,\neq)}

and not being influenced by
n_{(0,0)},
since

n_{0}-\partial_{y}P^{N_{2}}_{0}=\partial_{z}^{2}\triangle^{-1}n_{0}=\partial_{z}^{2}\triangle^{-1}n_{(0,\neq)}

and

\partial_{z}P^{N_{2}}_{0}=\partial_{y}\partial_{z}\triangle^{-1}n_{0}=\partial_{y}\partial_{z}\triangle^{-1}n_{(0,\neq)}.

As a consequence, we can decompose the zero mode
n_{0}
into
n_{0}=n_{(0,0)}+n_{(0,\neq)},
which satisfy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n_{(0,0)}-\frac{1}{A}\partial_{yy}n_{(0,0)}=-\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}\left(n_{(0,0)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}\right)+``{\rm good~terms}",\\
\partial_{t}n_{(0,\neq)}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle n_{(0,\neq)}=-\frac{1}{A}\Big(\partial_{y}\big(n_{(0,0)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,\neq)})+\partial_{z}\big(n_{(0,0)}\partial_{z}c_{(0,\neq)}\big)+\partial_{y}(n_{(0,\neq)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)})\\
\qquad\qquad+\partial_{y}\big(u_{2,(0,\neq)}n_{(0,0)}\big)+\partial_{z}(u_{3,(0,\neq)}n_{(0,0)})\Big)+``{\rm good~terms}".\end{array}\right.

(2.1)

Key observation 1. The introduction of energy estimates for
\frac{\partial_{z}}{\sqrt{1-\triangle}}n_{(0,\neq)}
.
Direct energy estimate for
n_{(0,\neq)}
shows that

\displaystyle\frac{A^{2\epsilon}}{2}\|n_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+A^{2\epsilon-1}\|\nabla n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+A^{2\epsilon-1}\big(\|n_{(0,0)}\partial_{z}c_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|n_{(0,0)}u_{3,(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+A^{2\epsilon-1}\big(\|n_{(0,0)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|n_{(0,0)}u_{2,(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|n_{(0,\neq)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{y}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\cdots

and for example, for the last term by Lemma
3.10
and (
A.1
) we have

\displaystyle\|n_{(0,\neq)}\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x,z}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\|n_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}n_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}L^{2}},

which implies that a smallness condition on
\|n_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}
seems to be necessary for
the estimate of
\|\nabla n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
. That is to say, in addition to the condition of the initial mass
M
in Theorem
1.2
, we also need to add a condition that requires a smallness of
\|(n_{\rm in})_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}}
. To remove this assumption,
a new observation is that
\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
is enough to close the estimates of both
\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}
and

\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}
, rather than
\|\nabla n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}.

In order to estimate
\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
, multiplying
(\ref{eq:n0'})_{2}
by
\frac{\partial_{zz}}{{1-\triangle}}n_{(0,\neq)}
or
\partial_{zz}c_{(0,\neq)}
one can obtain the estimate of
\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
.
With the help of this, we are able to completely remove the restriction on
\|(n_{\rm in})_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}}
(see Lemma
4.3
) and only need a restriction on initial cell mass
M.

The velocity
u_{1,0}
will be affected by the 3D lift-up effect.
Inspired by
[
13
]
, we decompose
u_{1,0}
into

u_{1,0}=\widehat{u_{1,0}}+\widetilde{u_{1,0}},
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\widehat{u_{1,0}}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\widehat{u_{1,0}}=-\frac{1}{A}\left(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\widehat{u_{1,0}}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\right)-u_{2,0},\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{u_{1,0}}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\widetilde{u_{1,0}}=-\frac{1}{A}\left(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\widetilde{u_{1,0}}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\widetilde{u_{1,0}}\right)-\frac{1}{A}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0},\\
\widehat{u_{1,0}}|_{t=0}=0,\quad\widetilde{u_{1,0}}|_{t=0}=(u_{1,\rm in})_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(2.2)

In this way,
\widetilde{u_{1,0}}
will not be affected by the 3D lift-up effect and will also remain unaffected by the linear energy transfer mechanism.
More importantly, if
A
is big enough, it can be regard as a perturbation, and we will explain it in estimating non-zero modes (Lemma
5.1
).

Given the above decompositions, it is natural to introduce the first energy functional

E_{1}(t)=E_{1,1}(t)+E_{1,2}(t)+E_{1,3}(t)
with

\displaystyle E_{1,1}(t)=

\displaystyle\|n_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+A^{\epsilon}\|\partial_{z}\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\frac{\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}+\|\partial_{z}^{2}n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{Y_{0}},

(2.3)

\displaystyle E_{1,2}(t)=

\displaystyle A^{\epsilon}\big(\|u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}

\displaystyle+\|\min\{(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}},1\}\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}\big),

\displaystyle E_{1,3}(t)=

\displaystyle A^{\epsilon}(A^{-1}{\|\widehat{u_{1,0}}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{4}}}+A^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\|\nabla\widehat{u_{1,0}}\|_{L^{2}H^{4}}}+\|\partial_{t}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\triangle\partial_{t}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}})

\displaystyle+A^{-\frac{1}{3}+\epsilon}(\|\widetilde{u_{1,0}}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\triangle\widetilde{u_{1,0}}\|_{Y_{0}}),

where
\epsilon
is a constant defined by

\epsilon=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\epsilon_{0},\quad\epsilon_{0}\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{4}{9}],\\
\frac{4}{9},\quad\epsilon_{0}>\frac{4}{9}.\end{array}\right.

\bullet

Constructing the energy functional of non-zero modes:

To facilitate the estimates of non-zero modes, we use the new vorticity
\omega_{2}=\partial_{z}u_{1}-\partial_{x}u_{3}
and new velocity
\triangle u_{2},
then

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n-\frac{1}{A}\triangle n=-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(un)-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-c=0,\\
\partial_{t}\omega_{2}+y\partial_{x}\omega_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\omega_{2}+\partial_{z}u_{2}=-\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3}),\\
\partial_{t}\triangle u_{2}+y\partial_{x}\triangle u_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle(\triangle u_{2})=\frac{1}{A}\partial_{x}^{2}n+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}^{2}n-\frac{1}{A}(\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})\\
\qquad+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}[\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})],\\
\nabla\cdot u=0.\end{array}\right.

(2.4)

Therefore, we can introduce the second energy functional

E_{2}(t)=E_{2,1}(t)+E_{2,2}(t)
with

\displaystyle E_{2,1}(t)=

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{2}n_{\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\partial_{z}^{2}n_{\neq}\|_{X_{a}},

\displaystyle E_{2,2}(t)=

\displaystyle A^{\frac{3}{4}\epsilon}(\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})+A^{-\frac{1}{3}+\frac{3}{4}\epsilon}(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\partial_{z}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}).

To estimate non-zero modes of the cell density, the third energy functional is introduced as follows:

\displaystyle E_{3}(t)=\|n\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}.

\bullet

Constructing the energy functional of non-zero modes with higher weight:

Considering

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\partial_{z}^{2}n_{\neq}+\Big(y+\frac{\widehat{u_{1,0}}}{A}\Big)\partial_{x}\partial_{z}^{2}n_{\neq}-\frac{\triangle\partial_{z}^{2}n_{\neq}}{A}=-\frac{\partial_{z}^{2}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\partial_{x}n_{\neq}}{A}-\frac{2\partial_{z}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\partial_{x}\partial_{z}n_{\neq}}{A}+``{\rm good~terms}".

Note that it is difficult to close the energy estimates by using
E_{1}(t),E_{2}(t),E_{3}(t)
due to the bad terms
\partial_{z}^{2}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\partial_{x}n_{\neq}
,

\partial_{z}\widehat{u_{1,0}}\partial_{x}\partial_{z}n_{\neq}
and the 3D lift-up effect. To overcome it, we introduce the fourth energy functional:

\displaystyle E_{4}(t)=\|\partial_{x}^{2}n_{\neq}\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}n_{\neq}\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}.

(2.5)

Lastly, one still needs the fifth energy functional to close the estimates of
E_{4}(t)
:

\displaystyle E_{5}(t)=A^{\frac{3}{4}\epsilon}\left(\|\partial^{2}_{x}u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}+\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}\right).

(2.6)

The fifth energy functional
E_{5}(t)
serves two important purposes: first, it is used to close the estimates of
E_{4}(t)
, and second, it is used to deal with the 3D lift-up terms in
(\ref{ini2})_{3}
and
(\ref{ini2})_{4}
.

Key observation 2. A new quasi-linearized decomposition.
One needs to note that
E_{5}(t)
is also related to
E_{4}(t)
, as there exists linear energy transfer mechanisms between
E_{4}(t)
and
E_{5}(t).

To estimate
E_{5},
it is important to
use the energy functional
E_{4}
and the new quantity
W=u_{2,\neq}+\frac{\partial_{z}\widehat{u_{1,0}}}{A+\partial_{y}\widehat{u_{1,0}}}u_{3,\neq}
as in
[
53
]
.
We introduce a quasi-linearized decomposition
W=W^{(1)}+W^{(2)},

satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\mathcal{L}_{V}W^{(1)}-2(\partial_{y}+\kappa\partial_{z})\triangle^{-1}(\partial_{y}V\partial_{x}W^{(1)})=2(\partial_{y}+\kappa\partial_{z})\triangle^{-1}(\partial_{y}V\partial_{x}W^{(2)})\\
+\frac{n_{\neq}-\partial_{y}P^{N_{2}}_{\neq}}{A}-\frac{\kappa\partial_{z}P^{N_{2}}_{\neq}}{A}+``{\rm good~part~1}",\\
\mathcal{L}_{V}W^{(2)}=(\partial_{t}\kappa-\frac{\triangle\kappa}{A})u_{3,\neq}-\frac{2}{A}\nabla\kappa\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq}+``{\rm good~part~2}",\\
W^{(1)}_{\rm in}=W_{\rm in},~~~W^{(2)}_{\rm in}=0.\end{array}\right.

In this way, we can estimate
E_{5}
directly without any other auxiliary terms:

E_{5}(t)\leq CA^{\frac{3}{4}\epsilon}\left(\|\partial_{x}\nabla W\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}+\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|_{X_{\frac{3}{2}a}}\right)\leq C\big(\|(\partial_{x}^{2}n_{\rm in})_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|(\partial_{z}^{2}n_{\rm in})_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+1\big).

(More details, see Lemma
7.2
and
7.3
)

2.3.
Main steps

Proof.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

\bullet

Step 1:
Let’s designate
T
as the terminal point of the largest range
[0,T]
such that the following
hypothesis hold

\displaystyle E_{1}(t)\leq 2E_{1},\ E_{2}(t)\leq 2E_{2},\ E_{3}(t)\leq 2E_{3},\ E_{4}(t)\leq 2E_{4},\ E_{5}(t)\leq 2E_{5},

(2.7)

for any
t\in[0,T]
, where
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
,
E_{3}
,
E_{4}
and
E_{5}
are constants independent of

t
and
A
and
will be decided during the calculation.

\bullet

Step 2:

We need to prove the following propositions:

Proposition 2.1
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.2
and the assumptions (
2.7
),
as long as

M<\frac{24\pi^{2}}{5},

there exists a positive constant
C_{(1)}
independent of
A
and
t,

such that if
A>C_{(1)},

E_{1}(t)\leq E_{1},

for all
t\in(0,T].

Proposition 2.2
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.2
and the assumptions (
2.7
),
there exists a positive constant
C_{(2)}
independent of
A
and
t,

such that if
A>C_{(2)},

E_{2}(t)\leq E_{2},

for all
t\in(0,T].

Due to
n=n_{\neq}+n_{(0,\neq)}+n_{(0,0)},

by Proposition
2.1
and Proposition
2.2
,
when
A>\max\{C_{(1)},C_{(2)}\}
,
we have

\|n\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\leq\|n_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|n_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|n_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\leq E_{1}+E_{2}.

Using the Moser’s iteration reported as in
[
15
]
and elliptic
estimates in Section
3
, we can prove that

E_{3}(t)\leq C(E_{1}^{6}+E_{2}^{6}+1)(E_{1}+E_{2}+||n_{\rm in}||_{L^{\infty}}+1):=E_{3},

for all
t\in(0,T].

Proposition 2.3
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.2
and the assumptions (
2.7
),
there exists a positive constant
C_{(3)}
independent of
A
and
t,

such that if
A>C_{(3)},

E_{4}(t)\leq E_{4},

for all
t\in(0,T].

Proposition 2.4
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.2
and the assumptions (
2.7
),
there exists a positive constant
C_{(4)}
independent of
A
and
t,

such that if
A>C_{(4)},

E_{5}(t)\leq E_{5},

for all
t\in(0,T].

\bullet

Step 3:

By the local well-posedness result in Theorem
B.1
,
there exists a time
T^{*}>0
such that a unique strong solution
(n,u)
to the system (
1.5
) exists on the interval
[0,T^{*}).
If
T^{*}<\infty
, by Proposition
2.1
-
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t<T^{*}}\|(\partial_{x}^{2},\partial_{z}^{2})n(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}+\|n(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C,

and next we will prove

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t<T^{*}}A^{-\frac{1}{12}}\|(\nabla u)(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C.

(2.8)

It follows from Lemma
3.13
and the bounded-ness of
E_{2}
that

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

which imply

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{12}}.

By (
3.24
) we have

\displaystyle\|{u_{1,0}}\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{u_{1,0}}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\widetilde{u_{1,0}}\|_{H^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{s}\widehat{u_{1,0}}(s)\|_{H^{2}}ds+\|\widetilde{u_{1,0}}\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CE_{1,3}A^{\frac{1}{3}-\epsilon}(1+A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t),

which and the bounded-ness of
E_{1}
imply that
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C.
Hence (
2.8
) holds.
Consequently, by the blow-up criterion (
B.1
) in Theorem
B.1
, the proof of Theorem
1.2
is complete. ∎

3.
Anisotropic Sobolev embeddings and a priori estimates

3.1.
Anisotropic Sobolev embeddings

Here, we give some anisotropic Sobolev inequalities based on the Fourier analysis. These inequalities play important roles in estimating nonlinear interaction terms.

Before the beginning, several important facts must be stated, which can be proven directly.
For a given function
f=f(x,y,z)
, by Fourier series there hold

\displaystyle\|f_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{j}f_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},\quad\|\partial_{x}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

(3.1)

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{z}^{j}f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},\quad\|\partial_{z}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{z}^{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where
j
is a positive constant with
j\geq 1
and
\|f\|_{L^{2}}
denotes
\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}
.

3.1.1.
Sobolev inequalities for the
L^{\infty}
norm

The following lemma can be used to estimate the
L^{\infty}
norm for the zero mode.

Lemma 3.1
.

For a given function
f(x,y,z)
and
f_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}{f}(t,x,y,z)dx,
we have

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right),

(3.2)

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\left(\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\right),

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Proof.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{1}
.

Thanks to the Fourier series
f_{0}=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)e^{ik_{3}z},
there holds

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\frac{1}{1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha}},

where we used (
A.1
) and
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}|k_{3}|^{2\alpha}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.3)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}\|k_{3}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Furthermore, by H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality again we get
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{1}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{2}
.

If we estimate (
3.3
) in another way:

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}^{2-2\alpha}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|k_{3}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

then one can prove
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{2}
directly.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{3}
.
For
f_{0}=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)e^{ik_{3}z},

we have

\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\frac{1}{1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, one immediately obtained
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{3}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_1}_{4}
.

Due to
\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)|^{2},
there holds

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}||\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)||^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

where we use (
A.1
) and

\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2},~\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma can be used to estimate the
L^{\infty}
norm for the non-zero mode.

Lemma 3.2
.

For a given function
g=g(x,y,z)
, if
g_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}{g}(t,x,y,z)dx=0,
then we have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{2}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big),

(3.4)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}g\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha},

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y,x}}\leq C(\|g\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}),

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}\leq\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big),

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}]
for
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{1}
and
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{8}
, and

\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1]
for others.

Proof.

Estimates of
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{1}
and
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{8}
.

Due to
g_{0}=0
, we denote
g(x,y,z)
by

\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{1}\neq 0}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y){\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)},

then by (
A.1
)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|k_{1}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|k_{1}\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}}{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|k_{1}k_{3}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|k_{1}^{2}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|k_{1}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}}{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}].

Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{2}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big).

Similarly, one can prove
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{8}
without the step of using (
A.1
).

Estimates of
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{2}
and
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{3}
.

Rewrite
g
into
g=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)e^{ik_{3}z},

and we have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|k_{3}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{1-\alpha}}{1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1]
.
Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality three times, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|k_{3}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{1-\alpha}}{1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|k_{3}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{4}}\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}},

thus

\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right),

which gives
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{2}
.

By rewriting
g
into
g(x,y,z)=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{1}\neq 0}\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)e^{ik_{1}x},

one can prove
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{3}
, similarly.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{4}
.

Denote
g(x,y,z)
by

\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{1}\neq 0}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y){\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)},

then

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}=\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}}{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|k_{1}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|k_{1}k_{3}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}}{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}(1+|k_{3}|^{\alpha})}.

By H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, we get

\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}g\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right).

Estimates of
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{5}
and
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{6}
.

Rewrite
g
into
g=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{1}\neq 0}\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)e^{ik_{1}x},

then

\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}_{y,z}}=\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0}\frac{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}_{y,z}}}{|k_{1}|^{\alpha}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, we obtain

\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}.

Similarly, by rewriting
g
into
g=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{g}_{k_{3}}(x,y)e^{ik_{3}z},
one can prove
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{6}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{7}
.

Denote
g(x,y,z)
by

\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y){\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)},

then

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

Using Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\Big(\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{7}
.

The proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma can be used to estimate the
L^{\infty}
norm for the z-part non-zero mode and the z-part zero mode.

Lemma 3.3
.

For a given function
f(x,y,z)
and
f_{(0,0)}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}}{f}(t,x,y,z)dxdz,
there hold

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}},

(3.5)

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\|\partial_{z}f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha},

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq\|\partial_{y}f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\partial_{y}f_{(0,0)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,0)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Proof.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{1}
.

Thanks to the Fourier series
f_{(0,\neq)}=\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{3}\neq 0}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)e^{ik_{3}z},
there holds

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\frac{|k_{3}|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|k_{3}\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}}{|k_{3}|^{\alpha}},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Using Hölder’s inequality, there holds

\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}},

which is
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{1}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{2}
.

Due to
\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{3}\neq 0}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}},

then there holds

\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\sum_{k_{3}\neq{0}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}=\sum_{k_{3}\neq{0}}\frac{\|k_{3}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}_{y}}}{|k_{3}|^{\alpha}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Using H
\rm\ddot{o}
lder’s inequality, we obtain
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{2}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{3}
.

Due to
\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{3}\neq 0}|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)|^{2},
there holds

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}||\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)||^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq|\mathbb{T}|\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{T}|\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{k_{3}\neq 0}\|\widehat{f}_{0,k_{3}}(t,y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\|\partial_{y}f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{3}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_3}_{4}
.
The last result follows from the 1D Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.1
).

The proof is complete.∎

The following lemma can be used to deal with the interaction between zero modes.

Lemma 3.4
.

For given functions
f(x,y,z)
and
g(x,y,z)
, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|g_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

(3.6)

\displaystyle\|f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\right)\|\partial_{y}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla(f_{0}g_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}})\|g_{0}\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\triangle(f_{0}g_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\|g_{0}\|_{H^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla\triangle(f_{0}g_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{H^{3}}\|g_{0}\|_{H^{3}},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Proof.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{1}
.

It follows from
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{1}
that

\displaystyle\|f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right)\|g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{2}
.
By
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{3}
and
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{4}
, we get

\displaystyle\|f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\leq\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\right)\|\partial_{y}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{2}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{3}
.
Given that
\|\nabla(f_{0}g_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\nabla f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|f_{0}\nabla g_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

using
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\nabla g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f_{0}\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right)\|\nabla g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}})\|g_{0}\|_{H^{1}},

using
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{3}
and
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{4}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\|\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\leq\|\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\right)\|\partial_{y}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{z}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}})\|g_{0}\|_{H^{1}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{3}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{4}
.
Thanks to Lemma
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\|\triangle(f_{0}g_{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\triangle f_{0}g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla f_{0}\cdot\nabla g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|f_{0}\triangle g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\triangle f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\nabla f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}L^{\infty}_{z}}^{2}\|\nabla g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\|\triangle g_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\|g_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}.

The proof of
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{5}
is similar to
\eqref{sob_result_4}_{4}
. The proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma can be used to deal with the interaction between non-zero modes.

Lemma 3.5
.

For given functions
f(x,y,z)
and
g(x,y,z)
satisfying
f_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}{f}(t,x,y,z)dx=0
and
g_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}{g}(t,x,y,z)dx=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\big)\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}f\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|f\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}f\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|f\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\Big(\|\partial_{y}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}f\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}f\|^{\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}f\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big)\|g\|_{L^{2}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1]
for the first three results and
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}]
for the last result.

Proof.

Using
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{3}
and
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{6},

there holds

\|fg\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\big)\|\partial_{x}g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}g\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_5}_{1}
.

Moreover, the inequality
\eqref{sob_result_5}_{2}
follows from
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{4}
and
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{7}.

\eqref{sob_result_5}_{3}
follows from
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{2}
and
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{5}.

\eqref{sob_result_5}_{4}
follows from
(\ref{sob_result_2})_{1}
.

The proof is complete. ∎

Lemma 3.6
.

For given functions
f(x,y,z)
and
g(x,y,z)
, it holds

\displaystyle\|(fg)_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.8)

\displaystyle\|(fg)_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\big)\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|(fg)_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{y}f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}f\|^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|f\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\right)\|g\|_{L^{2}},

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1].

Proof.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_6}_{1}
.
Let
f=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)e^{ik_{1}x+ik_{3}z}
and

g=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)e^{ik_{1}x+ik_{3}z},
then

(fg)_{(0,0)}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{-k_{1},-k_{3}}(y)g_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y).

Notice that by (
A.1
)

\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

then direct calculations yield that

\displaystyle\|(fg)_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1},-k_{3}}(y)\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1},-k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1},-k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_6}_{2}
.
Using
f=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{k_{1}}(y,z)e^{ik_{1}x}
and

g=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)e^{ik_{1}x},
we have

(fg)_{0}=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z).

Using
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{3}
and
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{4}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|(fg)_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}

(3.9)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big(\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\|^{\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\|^{1-\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha})\|g\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_6}_{2}
.

Estimate of
\eqref{sob_result_6}_{3}
.
For (
3.9
), if we use

\|(fg)_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\widehat{f}_{-k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\widehat{g}_{k_{1}}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}},
we can prove
\eqref{sob_result_6}_{3}
with the help of
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{1}.

The proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma is used to deal the interactions between the zero mode and the z-part non-zero mode.

Lemma 3.7
.

For given functions
f=f(x,y,z)
and
g=g(x,y,z)
, there hold

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,\neq)}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

(3.10)

\displaystyle\|f_{(0,0)}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,0)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1]
.

Proof.

Due to
f_{(0,\neq)}
is independent of the variable
x
, using
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{(0,\neq)}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}}\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\Big)\|f_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which implies
\eqref{sob_result_zz}_{1}
.

Due to
f_{(0,0)}
is independent of
x
and
z
, using
(\ref{sob_result_1})_{4}
, there holds

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{(0,0)}g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{z}}|f_{(0,0)}|^{2}dy\leq\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}\|f_{(0,0)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{(0,0)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which is
\eqref{sob_result_zz}_{2}
.
∎

3.2.
Elliptic estimates

The following elliptic estimates are necessary.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
c_{0}
and
n_{0}
be the zero mode of
c
and
n
, respectively, satisfying

-\triangle c_{0}+c_{0}=n_{0},

then there hold

\displaystyle\|\triangle c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|n_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\triangle c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{z}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{2}\triangle c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}^{2}\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{z}^{2}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|n_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

(3.11)

for any
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

The basic energy estimates yield

\displaystyle\|\triangle c_{0}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\nabla c_{0}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{0}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|n_{0}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which implies
\eqref{eq:elliptic}_{1}
.
Similarly, note that
\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}=\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},
and one can prove

\|\partial_{z}^{j}\triangle c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}^{j}\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

where
j=1,2.
Moreover,
using the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, we have

\|\nabla c_{0}(t)\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|\triangle c_{0}(t)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla c_{0}(t)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|n_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

which is
\eqref{eq:elliptic}_{4}
.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
c_{\neq}
and
n_{\neq}
be the non-zero mode of
c
and
n
,
respectively, satisfying

-\triangle c_{\neq}+c_{\neq}=n_{\neq},

then there hold

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{j}\triangle c_{\neq}(t

\displaystyle)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}^{j}\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{j}n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{j}\triangle c_{\neq}(t

\displaystyle)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}^{j}\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}\triangle c_{\neq}(t

\displaystyle)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{j}\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{j}n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{j}\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

where
j=0,1,2.

Proof.

By integration by parts, note that

\displaystyle\|\triangle c_{\neq}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{\neq}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|n_{\neq}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Using the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, we obtain

\|\nabla c_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|c_{\neq}(t)\|^{\frac{1}{8}}_{L^{2}}\|\triangle c_{\neq}(t)\|^{\frac{7}{8}}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|n_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}}.

Other results are similar and we omitted it.

∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
c_{(0,0)}
and
n_{(0,0)}
be the z-part zero mode of
c_{0}
and
n_{0}
,
respectively, satisfying

-\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}+c_{(0,0)}=n_{(0,0)},

then there hold

\displaystyle\|\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

(3.12)

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(3.13)

Proof.

A direct calculation yields that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which implies (
3.12
).

Due to

\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

thus

\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which implies (
3.13
).

∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
c_{(0,0)}
and
n_{(0,0)}
be the z-part zero mode of
c_{0}
and
n_{0}
,
respectively, satisfying

-\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}+c_{(0,0)}=n_{(0,0)},

then there holds

\displaystyle\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}}{4}.

Proof.

Multiplying
c_{(0,0)}
on both sides of
-\partial_{yy}c_{(0,0)}+c_{(0,0)}=n_{(0,0)},
the energy estimate shows that

\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=<n_{(0,0)}(t),c_{(0,0)}(t)>\leq\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{1}}.

By 1-D Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.1
), we
get

\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.14)

Therefore, there holds

\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|c_{(0,0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{1}}.

(3.15)

Using Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}}{4},

which along with (
3.14
) and (
3.15
) show that

\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\|\partial_{y}c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\|c_{(0,0)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{\|n_{(0,0)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{1}}}{4}.

∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
c_{(0,\neq)}
and
n_{(0,\neq)}
be the z-part non-zero mode of
c_{0}
and
n_{0}
,
respectively, satisfying

-\triangle c_{(0,\neq)}+c_{(0,\neq)}=n_{(0,\neq)},

then there hold

\displaystyle\|\triangle c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{j}\triangle c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\partial_{z}^{j}\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}^{j}c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where
j=1,2.

Proof.

Using energy estimates and Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality

\|\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\triangle c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq C\|n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|_{L^{2}},

we get the first two inequalities.

Direct calculations yield that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{j}\triangle c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\|\partial_{z}^{j}\nabla c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}^{j}c_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|\partial_{z}^{j}n_{(0,\neq)}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which is the last result.
∎

3.3.
Velocity estimates

Lemma 3.13
.

Assume that
u_{\neq}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}),
there hold

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

(3.16)

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x}^{2},\partial_{z}^{2})u_{3,\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}\nabla u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}\nabla u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}).

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\neq}=\partial_{z}u_{1,\neq}-\partial_{x}u_{3,\neq},

(3.17)

\displaystyle-\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}=\partial_{x}u_{1,\neq}+\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}.

Using the Fourier series, there hold

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\neq}=-\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(ik_{1}\hat{u}_{3,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)-ik_{3}\hat{u}_{1,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\right){\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)},

and

\displaystyle\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}=-\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(ik_{3}\hat{u}_{3,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)+ik_{1}\hat{u}_{1,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\right){\rm e}^{i(k_{1}x+k_{3}z)}.

Then

\displaystyle\|\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0,k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(k_{1}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\right)\Big(\|\hat{u}_{1,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\hat{u}_{3,k_{1},k_{3}}(y)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{y}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\|\partial_{x}u_{1,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}u_{1,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which implies
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{1}
. Moreover,
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{4}
can be proved by using

\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}+\partial_{z}\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}=-(\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})u_{3,\neq}.

And other inequalities are similar.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}|u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}),

(3.18)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{x}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}).

Proof.

Estimate of
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-neq0}_{1}
.
Recall the relation of (
3.1
), and by using
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{4}
in Lemma
3.2
and
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{2}
in Lemma
3.13
we have

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})^{2}.

Therefore, by
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{7}
and
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{3}
we have

\displaystyle\||u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{3}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{3})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

and

\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}|u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}).

Estimates of
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-neq0}_{2}
and
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-neq0}_{3}
.
Using Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
3.13
again, there hold

\displaystyle\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq C\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}},

(3.19)

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}^{2}\leq C(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}),

which implies

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{1,\neq}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}).

Thence,
\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}),
that is
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-neq0}_{2}
.
Similar to
(\ref{appa_1})
,
we also have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

(3.20)

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq C\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

then

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}u_{1,\neq}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u_{1,\neq}\partial_{x}^{2}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,\neq}\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
